But now I try to focus on it, and the numbers and words just scatter on me.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing your daily tasks because of this issue with your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, I am. I've actually had to let pretty much everything fall away lately. My church responsibilities, taking care of the garden, the youth maker program... I just can't keep up with them anymore. My wife and our pastor have had to step in and take over for me. To be honest, with the way my mind is right now, I just don't feel safe being out in my workshop by myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mind is wandering to other things when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Not exactly, no. It's not like my mind is wandering off to think about what's for dinner or what I need from the hardware store. It's more like... it just goes blank. I'm not thinking about other things, I just completely lose the thread of what's right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus, or is it more that you just can't seem to keep your attention on the task?\n\nPatient: It's really the second one. Like I was saying a minute ago, my mind just will not hold onto things anymore. I'm not getting distracted by what's going on around me in the room. It's just... when I sit down to try and write those simple notes for the youth program, the numbers and words just scatter on me. I just can't seem to keep my attention fixed on the task itself, even though it's something I used to be able to do in my sleep.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I just can't seem to keep my attention fixed on the task itself, even though it's something I used to be able to do in my sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to you when you're trying to focus on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose disjointed is a good word for it. Like I was just saying... when I try to write out those simple notes for the youth maker program, it's like my mind just won't hold onto the thoughts long enough to make sense of them. It's something I could do in my sleep before, but now... the words and numbers just scatter on me, and I just can't seem to string them together into a clear thought anymore.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been having trouble making decisions lately?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 67 / not_in_workforce / bachelors\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Diminished_Thinking\nmanifestation: \"I came in because my mind just will not hold onto things anymore—I sit down to write a simple note for the youth maker program, something I could do in my sleep before, and the numbers and words scatter on me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Plain patient.\nA plain patient describes their symptoms and experiences in a direct and straightforward manner, providing clear and relevant answers to the doctor's questions.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

Yes, very much so. I think it all goes back to what I've been saying about my mind just not holding onto things. It's not like my mind is wandering off to think about what's for dinner or what I need from the hardware store. It's more like... it just goes blank. To be honest, with the way my mind is right now, I just don't feel safe being out in my workshop by myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mind is wandering to other things when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Not exactly, no. It's not like my mind is wandering off to think about what's for dinner or what I need from the hardware store. It's more like... it just goes blank. I'm not thinking about other things, I just completely lose the thread of what's right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus, or is it more that you just can't seem to keep your attention on the task?\n\nPatient: It's really the second one. Like I was saying a minute ago, my mind just will not hold onto things anymore. I'm not getting distracted by what's going on around me in the room. It's just... when I sit down to try and write those simple notes for the youth program, the numbers and words just scatter on me. I just can't seem to keep my attention fixed on the task itself, even though it's something I used to be able to do in my sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to you when you're trying to focus on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose disjointed is a good word for it. Like I was just saying... when I try to write out those simple notes for the youth maker program, it's like my mind just won't hold onto the thoughts long enough to make sense of them. It's something I could do in my sleep before, but now... the words and numbers just scatter on me, and I just can't seem to string them together into a clear thought anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. I think it all goes back to what I've been saying about my mind just not holding onto things. When the words and numbers scatter on me like that—like with those youth program notes I mentioned—I can't even figure out what to do next. I just sit there unable to decide how to phrase a simple sentence or what step to take. I just can't process the information well enough to make a choice anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble finding the right words when you're trying to speak?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed that. My speech seems fine. It's more just the thinking part—getting my thoughts straight in my head—that's the problem. But once I know what I want to say, the words come out okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thinking has slowed down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. It's not just my thinking, either. Everything I do has slowed down. Whether it's just trying to get up out of my chair or trying to answer a question... it's like my whole body, and even my tongue, are just moving through wet cement. It just takes so much effort to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Are you needing help from others to complete your daily tasks due to your memory and focus issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I am. I've actually had to let a lot of things go completely. My church responsibilities, the garden... even that youth maker program I mentioned. I just can't do them anymore. My wife and our pastor have had to step in and take over for me. To be honest, I don't even feel safe being by myself out in my workshop right now. I just can't trust my mind to handle it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed since you started experiencing these problems with your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It's... well, most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling right in my chest. It just kind of sits there. Even when my wife is right next to me, talking kindly to me and trying to help... I just feel like I'm sitting at the bottom of a well. Just completely down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or feeling like you're withdrawing from friends and family because of your memory and focus issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been pulling back a lot. I just don't have any interest in being around people or doing the things I usually enjoy. I haven't even touched the wood pieces sitting on my workbench in weeks. I've completely skipped out on choir planning, and I haven't been to my chess games either. Honestly, even the thought of going fishing just... it sounds like way more effort than it's worth right now. And that's really not like me at all. I just don't care to do any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are not making sense to you or are difficult to follow when you're trying to think clearly?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. That's... well, that's really why I came in to get help, because my mind just will not hold onto things anymore. I'll sit down to write a simple note for that youth maker program—something I could have done in my sleep before. But now, when I try to think clearly, the numbers and words just scatter on me. I just can't follow my own thoughts.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

My wife and our pastor have had to step in and take over for me. To be honest, I don't even feel safe being by myself out in my workshop right now. I just can't trust my mind to handle it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed since you started experiencing these problems with your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It's... well, most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling right in my chest. It just kind of sits there. Even when my wife is right next to me, talking kindly to me and trying to help... I just feel like I'm sitting at the bottom of a well. Just completely down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or feeling like you're withdrawing from friends and family because of your memory and focus issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been pulling back a lot. I just don't have any interest in being around people or doing the things I usually enjoy. I haven't even touched the wood pieces sitting on my workbench in weeks. I've completely skipped out on choir planning, and I haven't been to my chess games either. Honestly, even the thought of going fishing just... it sounds like way more effort than it's worth right now. And that's really not like me at all. I just don't care to do any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are not making sense to you or are difficult to follow when you're trying to think clearly?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. That's... well, that's really why I came in to get help, because my mind just will not hold onto things anymore. I'll sit down to write a simple note for that youth maker program—something I could have done in my sleep before. But now, when I try to think clearly, the numbers and words just scatter on me. I just can't follow my own thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with family and friends since you started experiencing these problems with your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I just can't keep up with any of it. \n\nMy wife and our pastor have really had to step in to handle things for me. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my mind just will not hold onto things anymore. I'll sit down to write a simple note for the youth maker program I help with—something I could do in my sleep before—and... the numbers and words just scatter on me.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice that your mind couldn't hold onto things?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about three weeks now. I started noticing it right after... well, ever since my older sister died.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble remembering things that happened before your sister died?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any problem with that. Things from before she passed... they're still clear. It's really just the day-to-day stuff right now. Trying to hold onto what I'm doing in the moment is what's giving me trouble.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble learning new things, like instructions or new tasks?\n\nPatient: I haven't really tried to learn anything new lately, to be honest. I've just been trying to get through the things I already know how to do. So... I'm not really sure if I'd have trouble with new instructions or not. I just haven't had to do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble focusing on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's exactly the problem. That's actually the main reason I came in, because my mind just will not hold onto things anymore. I'll sit down to write a simple note for the youth maker program I help with... it's something I could do in my sleep before. But now I try to focus on it, and the numbers and words just scatter on me.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing your daily tasks because of this issue with your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, I am. I've actually had to let pretty much everything fall away lately. My church responsibilities, taking care of the garden, the youth maker program... I just can't keep up with them anymore. My wife and our pastor have had to step in and take over for me. To be honest, with the way my mind is right now, I just don't feel safe being out in my workshop by myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mind is wandering to other things when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Not exactly, no. It's not like my mind is wandering off to think about what's for dinner or what I need from the hardware store. It's more like... it just goes blank. I'm not thinking about other things, I just completely lose the thread of what's right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus, or is it more that you just can't seem to keep your attention on the task?\n\nPatient: It's really the second one. Like I was saying a minute ago, my mind just will not hold onto things anymore. I'm not getting distracted by what's going on around me in the room. It's just... when I sit down to try and write those simple notes for the youth program, the numbers and words just scatter on me. I just can't seem to keep my attention fixed on the task itself, even though it's something I used to be able to do in my sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to you when you're trying to focus on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose disjointed is a good word for it. Like I was just saying... when I try to write out those simple notes for the youth maker program, it's like my mind just won't hold onto the thoughts long enough to make sense of them. It's something I could do in my sleep before, but now... the words and numbers just scatter on me, and I just can't seem to string them together into a clear thought anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. I think it all goes back to what I've been saying about my mind just not holding onto things. When the words and numbers scatter on me like that—like with those youth program notes I mentioned—I can't even figure out what to do next. I just sit there unable to decide how to phrase a simple sentence or what step to take. I just can't process the information well enough to make a choice anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble finding the right words when you're trying to speak?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed that. My speech seems fine. It's more just the thinking part—getting my thoughts straight in my head—that's the problem. I just can't trust my mind to handle it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed since you started experiencing these problems with your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I just feel like I'm sitting at the bottom of a well. Just completely down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or feeling like you're withdrawing from friends and family because of your memory and focus issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been pulling back a lot.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nR41.0, R41.1, R41.2, R41.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because my mind just will not hold onto things anymore. I'll sit down to write a simple note for the youth maker program I help with—something I could do in my sleep before—and... the numbers and words just scatter on me.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice that your mind couldn't hold onto things?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about three weeks now. I started noticing it right after... well, ever since my older sister died.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble remembering things that happened before your sister died?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any problem with that. Things from before she passed... they're still clear. It's really just the day-to-day stuff right now. Trying to hold onto what I'm doing in the moment is what's giving me trouble.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble learning new things, like instructions or new tasks?\n\nPatient: I haven't really tried to learn anything new lately, to be honest. I've just been trying to get through the things I already know how to do. So... I'm not really sure if I'd have trouble with new instructions or not. I just haven't had to do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble focusing on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's exactly the problem. That's actually the main reason I came in, because my mind just will not hold onto things anymore. I'll sit down to write a simple note for the youth maker program I help with... it's something I could do in my sleep before. But now I try to focus on it, and the numbers and words just scatter on me.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing your daily tasks because of this issue with your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, I am. I've actually had to let pretty much everything fall away lately. My church responsibilities, taking care of the garden, the youth maker program... I just can't keep up with them anymore. My wife and our pastor have had to step in and take over for me. To be honest, with the way my mind is right now, I just don't feel safe being out in my workshop by myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mind is wandering to other things when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Not exactly, no. It's not like my mind is wandering off to think about what's for dinner or what I need from the hardware store. It's more like... it just goes blank. I'm not thinking about other things, I just completely lose the thread of what's right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus, or is it more that you just can't seem to keep your attention on the task?\n\nPatient: It's really the second one. Like I was saying a minute ago, my mind just will not hold onto things anymore. I'm not getting distracted by what's going on around me in the room. It's just... when I sit down to try and write those simple notes for the youth program, the numbers and words just scatter on me. I just can't seem to keep my attention fixed on the task itself, even though it's something I used to be able to do in my sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to you when you're trying to focus on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose disjointed is a good word for it. Like I was just saying... when I try to write out those simple notes for the youth maker program, it's like my mind just won't hold onto the thoughts long enough to make sense of them. It's something I could do in my sleep before, but now... the words and numbers just scatter on me, and I just can't seem to string them together into a clear thought anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. I think it all goes back to what I've been saying about my mind just not holding onto things. When the words and numbers scatter on me like that—like with those youth program notes I mentioned—I can't even figure out what to do next. I just sit there unable to decide how to phrase a simple sentence or what step to take. I just can't process the information well enough to make a choice anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble finding the right words when you're trying to speak?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed that. My speech seems fine. It's more just the thinking part—getting my thoughts straight in my head—that's the problem. But once I know what I want to say, the words come out okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thinking has slowed down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. It's not just my thinking, either. Everything I do has slowed down. Whether it's just trying to get up out of my chair or trying to answer a question... it's like my whole body, and even my tongue, are just moving through wet cement. It just takes so much effort to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Are you needing help from others to complete your daily tasks due to your memory and focus issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I am. I've actually had to let a lot of things go completely. My church responsibilities, the garden... even that youth maker program I mentioned. I just can't do them anymore. My wife and our pastor have had to step in and take over for me. To be honest, I don't even feel safe being by myself out in my workshop right now. I just can't trust my mind to handle it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed since you started experiencing these problems with your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It's... well, most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling right in my chest. It just kind of sits there. Even when my wife is right next to me, talking kindly to me and trying to help... I just feel like I'm sitting at the bottom of a well. Just completely down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or feeling like you're withdrawing from friends and family because of your memory and focus issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been pulling back a lot. I just don't have any interest in being around people or doing the things I usually enjoy. I haven't even touched the wood pieces sitting on my workbench in weeks. I've completely skipped out on choir planning, and I haven't been to my chess games either. Honestly, even the thought of going fishing just... it sounds like way more effort than it's worth right now. And that's really not like me at all. I just don't care to do any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are not making sense to you or are difficult to follow when you're trying to think clearly?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. That's... well, that's really why I came in to get help, because my mind just will not hold onto things anymore. I'll sit down to write a simple note for that youth maker program—something I could have done in my sleep before. But now, when I try to think clearly, the numbers and words just scatter on me. I just can't follow my own thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with family and friends since you started experiencing these problems with your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It's... well, it's put a lot of a burden on them. I've basically let everything fall away—my church responsibilities, taking care of the garden, that youth maker program I mentioned. I just can't keep up with any of it. \n\nMy wife and our pastor have really had to step in to handle things for me. And... honestly, my wife has to be around a lot more now, because I just do not feel safe being out in the workshop by myself anymore. They just have to do a lot more for me now. But as far as how they actually talk to me or act around me, I don't think anything else is different.\n\nDoctor: Are you unable to perform daily tasks without assistance due to your difficulty with focusing and holding onto thoughts?\n\nPatient: Yes, like I was just saying a moment ago, I really can't. It's not like my mind is wandering off to think about what's for dinner or what I need from the hardware store. It's more like... it just goes blank. I'm not thinking about other things, I just completely lose the thread of what's right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus, or is it more that you just can't seem to keep your attention on the task?\n\nPatient: It's really the second one. It's just... when I sit down to try and write those simple notes for the youth program, the numbers and words just scatter on me. I just can't seem to keep my attention fixed on the task itself, even though it's something I used to be able to do in my sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to you when you're trying to focus on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose disjointed is a good word for it. Like I was just saying... when I try to write out those simple notes for the youth maker program, it's like my mind just won't hold onto the thoughts long enough to make sense of them. It's something I could do in my sleep before, but now... the words and numbers just scatter on me, and I just can't seem to string them together into a clear thought anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble making decisions lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. I think it all goes back to what I've been saying about my mind just not holding onto things. When the words and numbers scatter on me like that—like with those youth program notes I mentioned—I can't even figure out what to do next. I just sit there unable to decide how to phrase a simple sentence or what step to take. I just can't process the information well enough to make a choice anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble finding the right words when you're trying to speak?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed that. Just completely down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or feeling like you're withdrawing from friends and family because of your memory and focus issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been pulling back a lot. I just don't have any interest in being around people or doing the things I usually enjoy. I haven't even touched the wood pieces sitting on my workbench in weeks. I've completely skipped out on choir planning, and I haven't been to my chess games either. I've actually had to let a lot of things go completely. My church responsibilities, the garden... even that youth maker program I mentioned. I just can't do them anymore. My wife and our pastor have had to step in and take over for me. To be honest, I don't even feel safe being by myself out in my workshop right now. I just can't trust my mind to handle it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed since you started experiencing these problems with your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It's... well, most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling right in my chest. It just kind of sits there. Even when my wife is right next to me, talking kindly to me and trying to help... I just feel like I'm sitting at the bottom of a well. Just completely down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or feeling like you're withdrawing from friends and family because of your memory and focus issues?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been pulling back a lot. I just don't have any interest in being around people or doing the things I usually enjoy. I haven't even touched the wood pieces sitting on my workbench in weeks. I've completely skipped out on choir planning, and I haven't been to my chess games either. Honestly, even the thought of going fishing just... it sounds like way more effort than it's worth right now. And that's really not like me at all. I just don't care to do any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are not making sense to you or are difficult to follow when you're trying to think clearly?\n\nPatient: Yes, very much so. That's... well, that's really why I came in to get help, because my mind just will not hold onto things anymore. I'll sit down to write a simple note for that youth maker program—something I could have done in my sleep before. But now, when I try to think clearly, the numbers and words just scatter on me. I just can't follow my own thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with family and friends since you started experiencing these problems with your mind?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It's... well, it's put a lot of a burden on them. I've basically let everything fall away—my church responsibilities, taking care of the garden, that youth maker program I mentioned. I just can't keep up with any of it. \n\nMy wife and our pastor have really had to step in to handle things for me. And... honestly, my wife has to be around a lot more now, because I just do not feel safe being out in the workshop by myself anymore. It definitely changes things when the people you care about have to step in and watch over you like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your family and friends interact with you since you started having these memory and focus problems?\n\nPatient: I think I mostly just covered that. Aside from my wife and the pastor stepping in to take over my responsibilities, and my wife keeping a closer eye on me... I haven't really noticed any other changes in how they treat me. They just have to do a lot more for me now. But as far as how they actually talk to me or act around me, I don't think anything else is different.\n\nDoctor: Are you unable to perform daily tasks without assistance due to your difficulty with focusing and holding onto thoughts?\n\nPatient: Yes, like I was just saying a moment ago, I really can't.
